PAR-089 (a diagnostics screen with no secrets or dictated content) is adjacent, not closed: no screen changes here.\n\n**Hardware UAT:** Y. The change touches the log calls on the heart path (capture, ASR, polish, insertion), so one ordinary take runs on the emulator after the change and its logcat is swept for the markers (a transcript fragment, an exception message) as evidence that the words never reach the log; the founder's phone is excluded by his instruction (2026-09-21 morning). What success looks like for a person: nothing they can see changes; a support engineer reading their logcat sees \"Polish failed (SocketTimeoutException at ProviderPolishClient.call:212)\", never the prompt or the words.\n\n## Preface — User Rubric\n\nUser Rubric: N/A — internal-only: no user-visible surface changes; the change is what the app writes into its own logs.\n\n## 0. TL;DR\n`DebugLogger` renders a `Throwable` as its class name and one frame location, never its message or trace; the dormant shared-storage file sink is deleted; every `${error.message}` in a diagnostic line becomes the exception's class name; the 43 raw `android.util.Log` calls in the paste package go through `DebugLogger`; the speech process's failure detail is the exception class, not its message; one JVM shape row over the source tree refuses the reintroduction of any of these, and marker rows prove the rendering.\n\n## 1. Problem\n`DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)` passes the throwable to `Log.e`, which prints `Throwable.toString()` (class plus MESSAGE) and the trace of every cause; a vendor or parser exception's message can carry the prompt, a URL with a key, or the dictated text (`code-gotchas.md` RULE: geniex-logs-the-prompt-so-silence-it-before-inference is the precedent). 29 diagnostic lines interpolate `${error.message}` directly. The off-by-default file sink writes to `/sdcard/EnviousWispr/debug.log` behind `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, which the manifest does not declare (`enableFileLogging` refuses at runtime); it has no caller and would write the same text to shared storage. 43 sites in `paste/` call `android.util.Log` directly, three of them with a throwable argument.\n\n## 2. Goals & non-goals\n### 2.1 Goals\n- Structurally: the ONLY renderer of a `Throwable` into a log line is `DebugLogger`, and it renders class + one frame, never message, `toString` or trace.\n- Every diagnostic line in `app/src/main` is free of `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `Throwable.toString` and string-templated throwables.\n- No shared-storage sink; no raw `android.util.Log` outside `DebugLogger`.\n- A static JVM row over the source tree keeps all three true; marker rows prove the renderer.\n### 2.2 Non-goals\n- Telemetry (`PayloadSanitizer`, Sentry, PostHog): unchanged, its own allowlist.\n- User-facing error text (`AppViewModel.historyError`, `DictionaryScreen`, the model-delivery notification): those read `failure.message` into a SCREEN, which is a product surface, not a diagnostic; out of scope, named in §14.\n- A closed enum of every diagnostic event name (the issue's \"closed event name\"): 180 sites of literal templates already read as shape; wrapping each in an enum member is the audit's -60 LOC budget times ten and pins nothing a shape row does not. The closed set this change enforces is the RENDERER's inputs (class name, frame, numbers, the literal template), not a catalogue of templates.\n- The vendor silencer (`S1NativeLog`, `geniex_log_silencer.cpp`): unchanged.\n\n## 2.5 Grounding brief\n\n### 1. Trace producer → owner → consumer, end to end\nProducers: 21 `DebugLogger.error` sites (`AsrService` 5, `AudioCaptureService` 7, `PolishService` 3, `SessionLog` 1, `SilenceVadService` 2, `SileroVadSession` 3), 111 `DebugLogger.warn`/`log`, 43 raw `Log.*` in `PasteAccessibilityService` (36), `RecordingAccessibilityOverlay` (5), `EditorInputSession` (2), plus the session owner's `SessionLog` seam (`DebugSessionLog` → `DebugLogger`) and `ProviderPolishClient`'s `logInfo`/`logWarn` lambdas. Owner of the rendering: `DebugLogger` (`android.util.Log` plus the file sink). Consumers: logcat (and the wispr-eyes harness, which greps template text: `Recording started`, `Take ended`, `Stopped by`, `insertion api=`), `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (a multiset of the audio service's templates), the coordinator rig's `FakeLog.awaitLine` (three literal lines). The message interpolations, measured on `69b4a6a`: 24 `DebugLogger`/`Log` lines and 5 `log.warn` lines (`grep -rn '\\.message' app/src/main/java | grep 'DebugLogger\\|Log\\.[iwe]\\|log\\.\\(warn\\|error\\|log\\)'`). One more producer of exception text into a diagnostic: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.message` as the AIDL failure `detail`, which the owner logs at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` (\"ASR failed: ... $detail\"); the legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` also carries it (a user-facing sentence on the OLD callback only; the versioned callback carries a code).\n### 2. Find the existing authority before proposing one\n`DebugLogger` is the one diagnostic owner; `SessionLog` is the owner's seam over it (#186). Both stay; the change narrows what `DebugLogger` renders. `scripts/check-visibility.py` is the precedent for a source-tree check; `SessionOwnerShapeTest.onlyTheOwnerPinsTheTarget` (an inventory of every call in production source) is the precedent for a JVM row that sweeps the tree.\n### 3. Read prior attempts and live direction\nThe catalog's `debug-local-log` row: Android's sink is off by default with no production caller and an undeclared storage requirement; macOS compiles every sink out of release; Windows ships a local log by design. No prior attempt to remove the Android sink; the audit (REF-10) asks for it.\n### 4. Name the lifecycle, trust and process boundaries a naive design would miss\nFour processes log; `DebugLogger` is an `object` per process, so the renderer is the same code everywhere. The AIDL `detail` string crosses `:asr` → main: making the PRODUCER send a class name makes every consumer content-free at once; the AIDL signature is unchanged (append-only rule untouched). The raw `Log` calls in `paste/` run in the default process's accessibility service; `DebugLogger` has no `Context` dependency, so the migration is a rename.\n### 5. Prove the high-risk premises\n- `Log.e(tag, msg, tr)` prints `tr`'s message: Android's `Log.e(String, String, Throwable)` appends `getStackTraceString(tr)`, which begins with `tr.toString()` = class + message, then every cause's. Verified by reading the platform contract; the marker row proves OUR renderer never calls it with a throwable.\n- No caller enables the file sink: `grep -rn enableFileLogging app/src` → one hit, the definition. The manifest declares no `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (grep, zero hits), so the sink could never have opened on a device.\n- The harness greps template text, not exception text: `scripts/uat/wispr_eyes.py` `last_take` reads `Stopped by ([^.]+)\\.`, `Recording started`, `insertion api=`; none of the migrated lines change a template the harness or a shape row reads (the templates stay; only `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` and the raw-Log → `DebugLogger` prefix change).\n\n## 3. Design\nD1. **`DebugLogger`** (`debug/DebugLogger.kt`): delete the file sink (`LOG_PATH`, `enableFileLogging`, `close`, `logWriter`, `writeToFile`, `truncateLog`, the `Environment`/`File`/`FileWriter` imports). `error(tag, message, throwable)` becomes `Log.e(tag, render(message, throwable))` with `render(message: String, throwable: Throwable?): String` (proposed) = `message` alone, or `\"$message ($ClassSimpleName at $frame)\"` where `frame` is the throwable's top frame as `ClassSimpleName.method:line` (or `\"no frame\"`), and the CAUSE chain contributes only class names (`caused by X, Y`), never a message. Add `debug(tag, message)` (proposed) → `Log.d` for the paste package's `Log.d` sites. `render` is `internal` so the marker rows call it on the JVM (the `Log` calls themselves are stubbed there).\nD2. **Every `${x.message}` in a diagnostic line → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}`** (29 sites, enumerated in §10). Where the same information is already carried by the throwable argument (`DebugLogger.error(TAG, \"…\", e)`) nothing changes.\nD3. **The paste package** (43 sites): `Log.i/w/d(TAG, …)` → `DebugLogger.log/warn/debug(TAG, …)`; `Log.w(TAG, \"…\", error)` (5 sites) → `DebugLogger.error(TAG, \"…\", error)`; the `import android.util.Log` lines go.\nD4. **The speech failure detail**: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.javaClass.simpleName` as `detail`; the owner's line at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` stays (it now prints a class name). The legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` (`detail.ifBlank { … }`) therefore reads the class name; that callback is the pre-#176 client path with no production caller (the versioned callback carries the code); stated, not redesigned.\nD5. **The static row** `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) (proposed, `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/`): reads every `app/src/main/java/**/*.kt`; (a) `import android.util.Log` appears only in `debug/DebugLogger.kt`; (b) no call to `DebugLogger.{log,warn,error,debug}`, `SessionLog`'s `log.{log,warn,error}`, or `logInfo`/`logWarn` carries `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `.toString()` of a throwable or `$e`/`$error`/`$it`-templated throwable in its argument text (the call's text is the balanced-paren span from the call token, so a multi-line call is one span); (c) `DebugLogger.kt` contains no `Log.e(` / `Log.w(` call with a third argument, no `stackTraceToString`, no `Environment`, no `FileWriter`. A two-way control: the row is run once against a fixture string containing each forbidden shape and must find every one.\nD6. **Marker rows** `DebugLoggerRenderTest` (proposed): `render(\"Polish failed\", RuntimeException(\"TRANSCRIPT_MARKER https://k=SECRET\"))` contains `RuntimeException`, contains a frame token, and contains neither marker; a cause chain `IllegalStateException(\"MARKER2\")` under it contributes the class name only; `render(\"x\", null)` is `\"x\"`.\n\n## 3b. Ownership justification\nNo new owner. `DebugLogger` stays the one renderer; its surface narrows (the sink gone, `render` added, `debug` added).\n\n## 4. Contract deltas\n- `DebugLogger.enableFileLogging()` / `close()`: deleted (no caller).\n- `DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)`: same signature; the rendering changes (class + frame, never message or trace).\n- `DebugLogger.debug(tag, message)`: new.\n- `IAsrCallback.onFailure(reason, detail)`: unchanged signature; `detail` now carries the exception's class name where it carried its message.\n- Log TEMPLATES: unchanged text where a template exists; the interpolated VALUE changes from a message to a class name in 29 lines.\n\n## 5. End-to-end state and lifecycle audit\nNo state. The file sink's `logWriter` lifecycle is deleted with it.\n\n## 6. Downstream consumer matrix\n- Logcat readers (a human, the harness): the same templates; a class name where a message was.\n- `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove`: its multiset lists templates with `${}` placeholders; the migrated lines keep their templates, so the row stays green (verified at build; a changed template is added there on purpose).\n- `FakeLog.awaitLine`: three literal lines, none carrying a message; unchanged.\n- The legacy `IAsrCallback` sentence: a class name instead of a message on two reasons (D4).\n- Telemetry: untouched.\n\n## 7. Failure-mode × caller table\n| Failure mode | Origin | Caller | What the user sees | Persisted | Retry |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| A throwable with a secret in its message reaches `DebugLogger.error` | a vendor SDK, a parser, OkHttp | any process | nothing; logcat shows the class and a frame | nothing | n/a |\n| A throwable with no stack frames (a synthetic one) | a test, a stripped build | `render` | \"(X at no frame)\" | nothing | n/a |\n| A new site interpolates `.message` | a future change | the static row | the unit suite is red with the file and line | n/a | fix the site |\n\n## 8. Caller-visible signals audit\nThe only signal that changes is the text of diagnostic lines: a class name replaces a message. Every user-facing sentence stays (`TakeNotices`, `InsertionOutcomeMessages`, the screens).\n\n## 9. Fallback source-of-truth audit\nNone: no fallback path reads a diagnostic.\n\n## 10. File-by-file changes\n- `app/src/main/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DebugLogger.kt`: D1.\n- `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` (29): `ui/PipelineBindings.kt:124`, `ui/EngineWarmUp.kt:51`, `ui/DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:277,1214,1317,1685`, `audio/DetectorFeed.kt:250,303,344`, `audio/TakeRoute.kt:113,204,245,329`, `audio/AudioCaptureService.kt:746,822,824,827,830`, `audio/WarmHoldOwner.kt:105,107,145`, `audio/PicturePublisher.kt:82,129`, `vad/SileroVadSession.kt:60`, `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt:667,688,1304,1411,1488` (these five also move to `DebugLogger`).\n- `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt`, `paste/RecordingAccessibilityOverlay.kt`, `paste/EditorInputSession.kt`: D3.\n- `asr/AsrService.kt:133,181`: D4.\n- `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DiagnosticsShapeTest.kt` (proposed, new), `DebugLoggerRenderTest.kt` (new).\n- `docs/audits/2026-09-22-194-*.md`; the catalog's `debug-local-log` Android row (the sink is gone).\n\n## 11. Testing\n1. Classes: `DebugLoggerRenderTest` is Product Outcome for the PRIVACY promise (`testing-philosophy.md` RULE: a-public-product-promise-needs-a-binding-test: the Data Safety form says no dictated content leaves the device, and a logcat line is readable by any app holding the platform's read-logs permission on a debug device and by anyone with the phone on a cable); `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) is a Drift Guard with a two-way control.\n2. Reverts: §11.2.\n3. Not tested: the vendor SDKs' own logging (owned by the silencer).\n### 11.1 Hardware UAT spec\nEmulator, wispr-eyes, debug build: one ordinary spoken take into Gmail (`dictate_emulator`) after the change; then `logcat -d` is swept for the dictated sentence's words and for `Exception:` followed by a colon-and-message shape from our tags (`DictationSession`, `AudioCapture`, `AsrService`, `PolishService`, `PasteService`): zero hits from our tags is the pass; the harness's own `insertion api=` and `Recording started` lines must still be found (the templates survived). The founder's phone: NOT RUN.\n### 11.2 Other obligations\n| Test | Class | Proves | Revert that turns it red |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.aThrowablesMessageNeverReachesTheLine` (proposed) | Product Outcome | the marker in the message and in a cause's message is absent; the class names and a frame are present | render `throwable.toString()` |\n| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.noThrowableRendersTheMessageAlone` (proposed) | Drift Guard | `render(\"x\", null) == \"x\"` | append a suffix |\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.onlyTheDiagnosticOwnerImportsAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | one file imports `android.util.Log` | add an import elsewhere |\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.noDiagnosticLineCarriesExceptionText` (proposed) | Drift Guard | zero forbidden shapes in any diagnostic call span; the fixture control finds all of them | reintroduce one `${e.message}` |\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.theOwnerNeverHandsAThrowableToAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | no three-argument `Log.e`/`Log.w`, no `stackTraceToString`, no file sink symbols in `DebugLogger.kt` | restore `Log.e(tag, message, throwable)` |\n| `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (exists) | Drift Guard | the audio templates unchanged | change a template |\n\n## 12. Blast radius & rollback\nEvery diagnostic line in four processes; no user-visible surface; no data. Rollback is the PR revert.\n\n## 13. Ship criteria specific to THIS change\n- Every row in §11.2 green; receipts red; the static row's two-way control found every fixture shape.\n- The emulator take's logcat sweep: zero content or exception-message hits from our tags; the templates the harness reads still present.\n- Codex all-clear with a confirming rerun.\n\n## 14. Open questions\n- The user-facing `failure.message` readers (`AppViewModel`, `DictionaryScreen`, `ModelDeliveryWorker`'s notification) show an exception's text ON SCREEN; that is a product-copy question (which sentence should a user see when history or the word list cannot be read), not a diagnostics one, and is not changed here; a follow-up issue is filed if Codex or the founder want it.\n\n## 15. Related\nREF-10 in `docs/audits/2026-09-20-senior-audit.md`; #176 (the typed ASR failure); `kotlin-patterns.md` RULE: no-content-in-diagnostics.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

a quarter of\n    56\t        // it is the conservative cap (review round 1). A normal screen is ~40 KB encoded.\n    57\t        const val MAX_ENCODED_BYTES = 256 * 1024\n    58\t    }\n    59\t}\n     1\tpackage com.envi.wispr.debug\n     2\t\n     3\timport android.content.BroadcastReceiver\n     4\timport android.content.Context\n     5\timport android.content.Intent\n     6\timport android.util.Log\n     7\timport com.envi.wispr.paste.PasteAccessibilityService\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Debug-only, no-audio insertion rig for synthetic UAT (#141). .claude/rules/testing-philosophy.md\\n     521 total\\n# #194 — Local diagnostics are structurally content-free\\n\\n## Preface — Lane + Hardware UAT declaration\\n\\n**Lane:** Code\\nmixed_pr: true — `Code` (`app/**`: `tests`, `codex-review`, `hardware-uat`) and `Docs/dev-tooling` (this plan, the audits, `.claude/**`: `cited-symbols` conditionally).\\n\\n**PAR rows closed:** none. PAR-089 (a diagnostics screen with no secrets or dictated content) is adjacent, not closed: no screen changes here.\\n\\n**Hardware UAT:** Y. The change touches the log calls on the heart path (capture, ASR, polish, insertion), so one ordinary take runs on the emulator after the change and its logcat is swept for the markers (a transcript fragment, an exception message) as evidence that the words never reach the log; the founder's phone is excluded by his instruction (2026-09-21 morning). What success looks like for a person: nothing they can see changes; a support engineer reading their logcat sees \\\"Polish failed (SocketTimeoutException at ProviderPolishClient.call:212)\\\", never the prompt or the words.\\n\\n## Preface — User Rubric\\n\\nUser Rubric: N/A — internal-only: no user-visible surface changes; the change is what the app writes into its own logs.\\n\\n## 0. TL;DR\\n`DebugLogger` renders a `Throwable` as its class name and one frame location, never its message or trace; the dormant shared-storage file sink is deleted; every `${error.message}` in a diagnostic line becomes the exception's class name; the 43 raw `android.util.Log` calls in the paste package go through `DebugLogger`; the speech process's failure detail is the exception class, not its message; one JVM shape row over the source tree refuses the reintroduction of any of these, and marker rows prove the rendering.\\n\\n## 1. Problem\\n`DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)` passes the throwable to `Log.e`, which prints `Throwable.toString()` (class plus MESSAGE) and the trace of every cause; a vendor or parser exception's message can carry the prompt, a URL with a key, or the dictated text (`code-gotchas.md` RULE: geniex-logs-the-prompt-so-silence-it-before-inference is the precedent). 29 diagnostic lines interpolate `${error.message}` directly. The off-by-default file sink writes to `/sdcard/EnviousWispr/debug.log` behind `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, which the manifest does not declare (`enableFileLogging` refuses at runtime); it has no caller and would write the same text to shared storage. 43 sites in `paste/` call `android.util.Log` directly, three of them with a throwable argument.\\n\\n## 2. Goals & non-goals\\n### 2.1 Goals\\n- Structurally: the ONLY renderer of a `Throwable` into a log line is `DebugLogger`, and it renders class + one frame, never message, `toString` or trace.\\n- Every diagnostic line in `app/src/main` is free of `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `Throwable.toString` and string-templated throwables.\\n- No shared-storage sink; no raw `android.util.Log` outside `DebugLogger`.\\n- A static JVM row over the source tree keeps all three true; marker rows prove the renderer.\\n### 2.2 Non-goals\\n- Telemetry (`PayloadSanitizer`, Sentry, PostHog): unchanged, its own allowlist.\\n- User-facing error text (`AppViewModel.historyError`, `DictionaryScreen`, the model-delivery notification): those read `failure.message` into a SCREEN, which is a product surface, not a diagnostic; out of scope, named in §14.\\n- A closed enum of every diagnostic event name (the issue's \\\"closed event name\\\"): 180 sites of literal templates already read as shape; wrapping each in an enum member is the audit's -60 LOC budget times ten and pins nothing a shape row does not. The closed set this change enforces is the RENDERER's inputs (class name, frame, numbers, the literal template), not a catalogue of templates.\\n- The vendor silencer (`S1NativeLog`, `geniex_log_silencer.cpp`): unchanged.\\n\\n## 2.5 Grounding brief\\n\\n### 1. Trace producer → owner → consumer, end to end\\nProducers: 21 `DebugLogger.error` sites (`AsrService` 5, `AudioCaptureService` 7, `PolishService` 3, `SessionLog` 1, `SilenceVadService` 2, `SileroVadSession` 3), 111 `DebugLogger.warn`/`log`, 43 raw `Log.*` in `PasteAccessibilityService` (36), `RecordingAccessibilityOverlay` (5), `EditorInputSession` (2), plus the session owner's `SessionLog` seam (`DebugSessionLog` → `DebugLogger`) and `ProviderPolishClient`'s `logInfo`/`logWarn` lambdas. Owner of the rendering: `DebugLogger` (`android.util.Log` plus the file sink). Consumers: logcat (and the wispr-eyes harness, which greps template text: `Recording started`, `Take ended`, `Stopped by`, `insertion api=`), `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (a multiset of the audio service's templates), the coordinator rig's `FakeLog.awaitLine` (three literal lines). The message interpolations, measured on `69b4a6a`: 24 `DebugLogger`/`Log` lines and 5 `log.warn` lines (`grep -rn '\\\\.message' app/src/main/java | grep 'DebugLogger\\\\|Log\\\\.[iwe]\\\\|log\\\\.\\\\(warn\\\\|error\\\\|log\\\\)'`). One more producer of exception text into a diagnostic: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.message` as the AIDL failure `detail`, which the owner logs at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` (\\\"ASR failed: ... $detail\\\"); the legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` also carries it (a user-facing sentence on the OLD callback only; the versioned callback carries a code).\\n### 2. Find the existing authority before proposing one\\n`DebugLogger` is the one diagnostic owner; `SessionLog` is the owner's seam over it (#186). Both stay; the change narrows what `DebugLogger` renders. `scripts/check-visibility.py` is the precedent for a source-tree check; `SessionOwnerShapeTest.onlyTheOwnerPinsTheTarget` (an inventory of every call in production source) is the precedent for a JVM row that sweeps the tree.\\n### 3. Read prior attempts and live direction\\nThe catalog's `debug-local-log` row: Android's sink is off by default with no production caller and an undeclared storage requirement; macOS compiles every sink out of release; Windows ships a local log by design. No prior attempt to remove the Android sink; the audit (REF-10) asks for it.\\n### 4. Name the lifecycle, trust and process boundaries a naive design would miss\\nFour processes log; `DebugLogger` is an `object` per process, so the renderer is the same code everywhere. The AIDL `detail` string crosses `:asr` → main: making the PRODUCER send a class name makes every consumer content-free at once; the AIDL signature is unchanged (append-only rule untouched). The raw `Log` calls in `paste/` run in the default process's accessibility service; `DebugLogger` has no `Context` dependency, so the migration is a rename.\\n### 5. Prove the high-risk premises\\n- `Log.e(tag, msg, tr)` prints `tr`'s message: Android's `Log.e(String, String, Throwable)` appends `getStackTraceString(tr)`, which begins with `tr.toString()` = class + message, then every cause's. Verified by reading the platform contract; the marker row proves OUR renderer never calls it with a throwable.\\n- No caller enables the file sink: `grep -rn enableFileLogging app/src` → one hit, the definition. The manifest declares no `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (grep, zero hits), so the sink could never have opened on a device.\\n- The harness greps template text, not exception text: `scripts/uat/wispr_eyes.py` `last_take` reads `Stopped by ([^.]+)\\\\.`, `Recording started`, `insertion api=`; none of the migrated lines change a template the harness or a shape row reads (the templates stay; only `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` and the raw-Log → `DebugLogger` prefix change).\\n\\n## 3. Design\\nD1. **`DebugLogger`** (`debug/DebugLogger.kt`): delete the file sink (`LOG_PATH`, `enableFileLogging`, `close`, `logWriter`, `writeToFile`, `truncateLog`, the `Environment`/`File`/`FileWriter` imports). `error(tag, message, throwable)` becomes `Log.e(tag, render(message, throwable))` with `render(message: String, throwable: Throwable?): String` (proposed) = `message` alone, or `\\\"$message ($ClassSimpleName at $frame)\\\"` where `frame` is the throwable's top frame as `ClassSimpleName.method:line` (or `\\\"no frame\\\"`), and the CAUSE chain contributes only class names (`caused by X, Y`), never a message. Add `debug(tag, message)` (proposed) → `Log.d` for the paste package's `Log.d` sites. `render` is `internal` so the marker rows call it on the JVM (the `Log` calls themselves are stubbed there).\\nD2. **Every `${x.message}` in a diagnostic line → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}`** (29 sites, enumerated in §10). Where the same information is already carried by the throwable argument (`DebugLogger.error(TAG, \\\"…\\\", e)`) nothing changes.\\nD3. **The paste package** (43 sites): `Log.i/w/d(TAG, …)` → `DebugLogger.log/warn/debug(TAG, …)`; `Log.w(TAG, \\\"…\\\", error)` (5 sites) → `DebugLogger.error(TAG, \\\"…\\\", error)`; the `import android.util.Log` lines go.\\nD4. **The speech failure detail**: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.javaClass.simpleName` as `detail`; the owner's line at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` stays (it now prints a class name). The legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` (`detail.ifBlank { … }`) therefore reads the class name; that callback is the pre-#176 client path with no production caller (the versioned callback carries the code); stated, not redesigned.\\nD5. **The static row** `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) (proposed, `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/`): reads every `app/src/main/java/**/*.kt`; (a) `import android.util.Log` appears only in `debug/DebugLogger.kt`; (b) no call to `DebugLogger.{log,warn,error,debug}`, `SessionLog`'s `log.{log,warn,error}`, or `logInfo`/`logWarn` carries `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `.toString()` of a throwable or `$e`/`$error`/`$it`-templated throwable in its argument text (the call's text is the balanced-paren span from the call token, so a multi-line call is one span); (c) `DebugLogger.kt` contains no `Log.e(` / `Log.w(` call with a third argument, no `stackTraceToString`, no `Environment`, no `FileWriter`. A two-way control: the row is run once against a fixture string containing each forbidden shape and must find every one.\\nD6. **Marker rows** `DebugLoggerRenderTest` (proposed): `render(\\\"Polish failed\\\", RuntimeException(\\\"TRANSCRIPT_MARKER https://k=SECRET\\\"))` contains `RuntimeException`, contains a frame token, and contains neither marker; a cause chain `IllegalStateException(\\\"MARKER2\\\")` under it contributes the class name only; `render(\\\"x\\\", null)` is `\\\"x\\\"`.\\n\\n## 3b. Ownership justification\\nNo new owner. `DebugLogger` stays the one renderer; its surface narrows (the sink gone, `render` added, `debug` added).\\n\\n## 4. Contract deltas\\n- `DebugLogger.enableFileLogging()` / `close()`: deleted (no caller).\\n- `DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)`: same signature; the rendering changes (class + frame, never message or trace).\\n- `DebugLogger.debug(tag, message)`: new.\\n- `IAsrCallback.onFailure(reason, detail)`: unchanged signature; `detail` now carries the exception's class name where it carried its message.\\n- Log TEMPLATES: unchanged text where a template exists; the interpolated VALUE changes from a message to a class name in 29 lines.\\n\\n## 5. End-to-end state and lifecycle audit\\nNo state. The file sink's `logWriter` lifecycle is deleted with it.\\n\\n## 6. Downstream consumer matrix\\n- Logcat readers (a human, the harness): the same templates; a class name where a message was.\\n- `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove`: its multiset lists templates with `${}` placeholders; the migrated lines keep their templates, so the row stays green (verified at build; a changed template is added there on purpose).\\n- `FakeLog.awaitLine`: three literal lines, none carrying a message; unchanged.\\n- The legacy `IAsrCallback` sentence: a class name instead of a message on two reasons (D4).\\n- Telemetry: untouched.\\n\\n## 7. Failure-mode × caller table\\n| Failure mode | Origin | Caller | What the user sees | Persisted | Retry |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| A throwable with a secret in its message reaches `DebugLogger.error` | a vendor SDK, a parser, OkHttp | any process | nothing; logcat shows the class and a frame | nothing | n/a |\\n| A throwable with no stack frames (a synthetic one) | a test, a stripped build | `render` | \\\"(X at no frame)\\\" | nothing | n/a |\\n| A new site interpolates `.message` | a future change | the static row | the unit suite is red with the file and line | n/a | fix the site |\\n\\n## 8. Caller-visible signals audit\\nThe only signal that changes is the text of diagnostic lines: a class name replaces a message. Every user-facing sentence stays (`TakeNotices`, `InsertionOutcomeMessages`, the screens).\\n\\n## 9. Fallback source-of-truth audit\\nNone: no fallback path reads a diagnostic.\\n\\n## 10. File-by-file changes\\n- `app/src/main/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DebugLogger.kt`: D1.\\n- `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` (29): `ui/PipelineBindings.kt:124`, `ui/EngineWarmUp.kt:51`, `ui/DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:277,1214,1317,1685`, `audio/DetectorFeed.kt:250,303,344`, `audio/TakeRoute.kt:113,204,245,329`, `audio/AudioCaptureService.kt:746,822,824,827,830`, `audio/WarmHoldOwner.kt:105,107,145`, `audio/PicturePublisher.kt:82,129`, `vad/SileroVadSession.kt:60`, `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt:667,688,1304,1411,1488` (these five also move to `DebugLogger`).\\n- `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt`, `paste/RecordingAccessibilityOverlay.kt`, `paste/EditorInputSession.kt`: D3.\\n- `asr/AsrService.kt:133,181`: D4.\\n- `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DiagnosticsShapeTest.kt` (proposed, new), `DebugLoggerRenderTest.kt` (new).\\n- `docs/audits/2026-09-22-194-*.md`; the catalog's `debug-local-log` Android row (the sink is gone).\\n\\n## 11. Testing\\n1. Classes: `DebugLoggerRenderTest` is Product Outcome for the PRIVACY promise (`testing-philosophy.md` RULE: a-public-product-promise-needs-a-binding-test: the Data Safety form says no dictated content leaves the device, and a logcat line is readable by any app holding the platform's read-logs permission on a debug device and by anyone with the phone on a cable); `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) is a Drift Guard with a two-way control.\\n2. Reverts: §11.2.\\n3. Not tested: the vendor SDKs' own logging (owned by the silencer).\\n### 11.1 Hardware UAT spec\\nEmulator, wispr-eyes, debug build: one ordinary spoken take into Gmail (`dictate_emulator`) after the change; then `logcat -d` is swept for the dictated sentence's words and for `Exception:` followed by a colon-and-message shape from our tags (`DictationSession`, `AudioCapture`, `AsrService`, `PolishService`, `PasteService`): zero hits from our tags is the pass; the harness's own `insertion api=` and `Recording started` lines must still be found (the templates survived). The founder's phone: NOT RUN.\\n### 11.2 Other obligations\\n| Test | Class | Proves | Revert that turns it red |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.aThrowablesMessageNeverReachesTheLine` (proposed) | Product Outcome | the marker in the message and in a cause's message is absent; the class names and a frame are present | render `throwable.toString()` |\\n| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.noThrowableRendersTheMessageAlone` (proposed) | Drift Guard | `render(\\\"x\\\", null) == \\\"x\\\"` | append a suffix |\\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.onlyTheDiagnosticOwnerImportsAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | one file imports `android.util.Log` | add an import elsewhere |\\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.noDiagnosticLineCarriesExceptionText` (proposed) | Drift Guard | zero forbidden shapes in any diagnostic call span; the fixture control finds all of them | reintroduce one `${e.message}` |\\n| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.theOwnerNeverHandsAThrowableToAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | no three-argument `Log.e`/`Log.w`, no `stackTraceToString`, no file sink symbols in `DebugLogger.kt` | restore `Log.e(tag, message, throwable)` |\\n| `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (exists) | Drift Guard | the audio templates unchanged | change a template |\\n\\n## 12. Blast radius & rollback\\nEvery diagnostic line in four processes; no user-visible surface; no data. Rollback is the PR revert.\\n\\n## 13. Ship criteria specific to THIS change\\n- Every row in §11.2 green; receipts red; the static row's two-way control found every fixture shape.\\n- The emulator take's logcat sweep: zero content or exception-message hits from our tags; the templates the harness reads still present.\\n- Codex all-clear with a confirming rerun.\\n\\n## 14. Open questions\\n- The user-facing `failure.message` readers (`AppViewModel`, `DictionaryScreen`, `ModelDeliveryWorker`'s notification) show an exception's text ON SCREEN; that is a product-copy question (which sentence should a user see when history or the word list cannot be read), not a diagnostics one, and is not changed here; a follow-up issue is filed if Codex or the founder want it.\\n\\n## 15. Each dictation can run up to ten minutes. Bring your own key only if you want cloud polish.\ndocs/play-listing/store-listing.md:51:EnviousWispr asks for Android's Accessibility permission so it can find the text box you are using and put your words there after you start a dictation. It is a dictation app, not an assistive tool. It never operates your phone on its own, and field contents are never sent to Envious Labs. The optional cloud polish needs a\ndocs/play-listing/console-declarations.md:104:No contacts, SMS, call log, location or file permissions are requested (manifest:\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:17:EnviousWispr for Android is a mobile application for on-device voice-to-text dictation. Users tap a floating button, a Quick Settings tile, or a notification, speak, and the app transcribes their speech into text using machine learning models that run on the phone, then places the result in the text field the user was typing in. Audio capture and speech-to-text transcription run locally on the user's phone. EnviousWispr does not operate a transcription server, and the app is designed so that the user's dictation audio and transcripts are not sent to Envious Labs through the transcription or AI polish features. Polishing the transcribed text is optional and runs on the phone by default; if the user chooses a cloud polish provider, the transcribed text, together with the user's custom words list (which may include names the user has added), is sent (never the audio) from the user's phone directly to that provider, such as OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google Gemini (or, for a configuration saved by an earlier version, a server the user hosts), under the user's own API key and that provider's terms. The Android app requests the Android Accessibility permission solely to identify the text field the user is working in and to insert the transcribed text there after the user starts a dictation; the contents of that field are processed on the phone and are not sent to Envious Labs. The Android app uses the microphone only while the user is dictating. The app downloads its speech and polish models once, over the internet, from a model host; that download carries no user content. The Android app sends pseudonymous usage and crash reports to Envious Labs' service providers, PostHog (usage) and Sentry (crash reports), both hosted in the United States: whether a dictation finished and how long each step took, which settings are on, which app the words were placed in, model download outcomes, and a crash report when the app fails. PostHog may derive an approximate city or region from the request IP address; the app does not access the device's location. These reports are designed never to contain audio, dictated text, the contents of any text field, names entered by the user, file contents, or API keys. Crash reports may include sanitized technical stack-frame locations. Each report carries a random installation identifier generated on the phone that distinguishes one installation from another and is not linked to an account. The Android app creates no account. Dictation history, custom words, and settings are stored on the phone only and can be deleted by the user in the app or by uninstalling it.\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:27:Every sentence traces to code in the Android repo: the entry points and on-device transcription\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:29:`docs/play-data-safety-answers.md`), the accessibility purpose and the field-contents promise\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:30:(`ui/AccessibilityGuideActivity.kt`, `.claude/rules/content-brand.md` RULE:\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:41:listing > Privacy policy, and into Policy > App content > Privacy policy.\ndocs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md:17:EnviousWispr for Android is a mobile application for on-device voice-to-text dictation. Users tap a floating button, a Quick Settings tile, or a notification, speak, and the app transcribes their speech into text using machine learning models that run on the phone, then places the result in the text field the user was typing in. Audio capture and speech-to-text transcription run locally on the user's phone. EnviousWispr does not operate a transcription server, and the app is designed so that the user's dictation audio and transcripts are not sent to Envious Labs through the transcription or AI polish features. Polishing the transcribed text is optional and runs on the phone by default; if the user chooses a cloud polish provider, the transcribed text, together with the user's custom words list (which may include names the user has added), is sent (never the audio) from the user's phone directly to that provider, such as OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google Gemini (or, for a configuration saved by an earlier version, a server the user hosts), under the user's own API key and that provider's terms. The Android app requests the Android Accessibility permission solely to identify the text field the user is working in and to insert the transcribed text there after the user starts a dictation; the contents of that field are processed on the phone and are not sent to Envious Labs. The Android app uses the microphone only while the user is dictating. The app downloads its speech and polish models once, over the internet, from a model host; that download carries no user content. The Android app sends pseudonymous usage and crash reports to Envious Labs' service providers, PostHog (usage) and Sentry (crash reports), both hosted in the United States: whether a dictation finished and how long each step took, which settings are on, which app the words were placed in, model download outcomes, and a crash report when the app fails. PostHog may derive an approximate city or region from the request IP address; the app does not access the device's location. These reports are designed never to contain audio, dictated text, the contents of any text field, names entered by the user, file contents, or API keys. Verified against the tree\n     5\t2026-09-20 (telemetry added by #176). Owner of the code truth: `app/src/main/java/com/envi/wispr/privacy/PrivacyDisclosure.kt`\n     6\t(the sentences) with `app/src/main/java/com/envi/wispr/telemetry/PayloadSanitizer.kt` (what a row may carry).\n     7\tThis is not required for an internal-only release; it gates the first closed or public track.\n     8\t\n     9\t## FACT: what-the-app-actually-sends\n    10\tGrounded 2026-09-15. `INTERNET` is the only network permission (`AndroidManifest.xml`). Outbound hosts in\n    11\tthe code:\n    12\t\n    13\t- `huggingface.co` — the two model files at a pinned revision, `models/ModelManifest.kt`. No user content.\n    14\t- `api.openai.com`, `api.anthropic.com`, `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, and the user's own\n    15\t  self-hosted endpoint — cloud polish ONLY, reached only when the user turns on cloud polish and supplies\n    16\t  their own key. The selected transcript TEXT plus the custom-words list is sent, straight to the provider\n    17\t  the user chose, under the user's own key. `providers/ProviderPolishClient.kt` sends it through\n    18\t  `providers/HttpProviderTransport.kt`; the exact body per provider is `providers/OpenAiAdapter.kt`,\n    19\t  `GeminiAdapter.kt`, `ClaudeAdapter.kt`, `SelfHostedAdapter.kt`; the prompt is\n    20\t  `providers/ProviderPolishPrompt.kt`.\n    21\t\n    22\t- `us.i.posthog.com` (usage rows) and `o4511097055477760.ingest.us.sentry.io` (crash reports and our own\n    23\t  defects), since #176. Content-free by construction: every property leaves only under a name and a shape the\n    24\t  allowlist in `telemetry/PayloadSanitizer.kt` declares (closed tokens, numbers, booleans, a UUID, a package\n    25\t  name, the device model); a transcript, a product name, a key, an email or a path cannot pass, and exception\n    26\t  messages are always dropped. One random install id (`telemetry/InstallIdentity.kt`) tells installs apart; it\n    27\t  is minted on the phone, never derived from the person or the account, and never reset. What the rows say is\n    28\t  in `.claude/knowledge/telemetry.md`.\n    29\t\n    30\tThere is NO Envious Labs server. Audio never leaves the phone on any path. Provider API keys are stored in the\n    31\tAndroid Keystore and are never sent to us (`providers/AndroidKeystoreSecretStore.kt`). Play builds carry the\n    32\ttwo client keys from repository variables; a PR build has none and sends nothing (`scripts/release/build.sh`).\n    33\t\n    34\t## FACT: the-form-answers\n    35\tGoogle defines \"collected\" as data leaving the device (including via an integrated third party) and\n    36\t\"shared\" as transfer to a third party. Cloud polish is a user-initiated transfer, but because an enabled\n    37\tcloud-polish setting sends every subsequent transcript automatically, declare it as collected AND shared\n    38\trather than lean on the user-initiated-sharing exception (the conservative, review-safe answer; council\n    39\t2026-09-15).\n    40\t\n    41\t| Form section | Answer | Why |\n    42\t|---|---|---|\n    43\t| Does your app collect or share any of the required user data types? | **Yes** | Cloud polish transmits transcript text off-device. |\n    44\t| Data type: **Messages / Other in-app messages** (the transcript text) | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **Yes**. Optional. Purpose: **App functionality**. | Only when the user enables cloud polish under their own key. |\n    45\t| Data type: **Audio / Voice or sound recordings** | Collected: **No**. Shared: **No**. | Audio never leaves the phone. |\n    46\t| Data type: **App activity / App interactions** | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **No**. Required (no in-app switch). Purpose: **Analytics**. Not ephemeral. | The usage rows (#176): a dictation's outcome and timings, settings changes, onboarding steps, model downloads. Sent to PostHog, a processor acting for us, which Google does not count as \"sharing\". |\n    47\t| Data type: **App info and performance / Crash logs** | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **No**. Required. Purpose: **Analytics**. Not ephemeral. | Crash reports and our own defect reports to Sentry, a processor. No message text; frames only. |\n    48\t| Data type: **App info and performance / Diagnostics** | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **No**. Required. Purpose: **Analytics**. Not ephemeral. | Step timings and the take's route and ending, on the same usage rows. |\n    49\t| Data type: **Device or other IDs** | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **No**. Required. Purpose: **Analytics**. Not ephemeral. | The random install id on every row, plus the device model and OS version. Google counts an app-instance id here. |\n    50\t| Data type: **Location / Approximate location** | Collected: **Yes**. Shared: **No**. Required. Purpose: **Analytics**. Not ephemeral. | PostHog, as our processor, derives an approximate city or region from the telemetry request's IP (a project-level setting shared with the Mac). The app never reads GPS or the device's location. Google requires this to be declared. |\n    51\t| Data type: **Contacts, Personal info, Financial, Health, Photos, Files, Calendar, Web browsing** | **No** to all. | Nothing of the kind is read. On-device processing is exempt. |\n    52\t| Is all collected data encrypted in transit? | **Yes** | Model downloads, both telemetry vendors and every provider endpoint are HTTPS. |\n    53\t| Do you provide a way to request data deletion? | **No** | There is no account, and the app does not yet show the random install id that would locate one installation's rows reliably (an approximate date and device cannot). On-device History, custom words and settings are deleted in the app or by uninstalling. Stage 2: an in-app \"copy my install id\" row turns this into a Yes. |\n    54\t| Privacy policy URL | **Required** (a public HTTPS non-PDF page). | Mandatory even when we collect nothing ourselves, because the app requests the Accessibility API. |\n    55\t\n    56\t## FACT: the-listing-and-policy-must-agree\n    57\tThe four claims must match `PrivacyDisclosure.kt`: audio never leaves the phone (true on every path);\n    58\tselected TEXT goes to the user's chosen cloud provider under their key (true, and must appear); usage and\n    59\tcrash reports leave with no dictated words, entered names, file contents or keys, under a random install id,\n    60\twith an approximate location derived by PostHog from the IP (true since #176, and must appear: the old \"no\n    61\ttracking, no analytics\" and \"anonymous\" wordings are false); NPU polish is not shipped (keep it out). A listing\n    62\tcarrying only the audio claim answers this form wrongly (`../.claude/rules/content-brand.md` RULE:\n    63\tthe-listing-may-only-claim-what-ships).\n    64\t\n    65\t## FACT: the-foreground-service-and-accessibility-declarations-are-separate\n    66\tBeyond Data Safety, two Console declarations are still owed and are the highest review risk:\n    67\t\n    68\t- **Foreground service types.** The manifest declares `microphone|dataSync` on the dictation service and\n    69\t  `dataSync` on model delivery (`AndroidManifest.xml`). Android 14+ requires a Console declaration for each\n    70\t  type with a short use description and, for microphone, a demo.\n    71\t- **Accessibility API.** `isAccessibilityTool` must be declared **false** (this is a general dictation app,\n    72\t  not an assistive tool), plus the prominent-disclosure screen (already built,\n    73\t  `ui/AccessibilityGuideActivity.kt`), plus a demo video showing the disclosure, the Agree choice, the\n    74\t  settings hand-off, and cross-app insertion. Owner: `../.claude/rules/content-brand.md`\n    75\t  RULE: the-accessibility-disclosure-is-policy-not-copy.\n     1\t# Play Store Readiness\n     2\t\n     3\tThe path from today's build to a public Google Play release. Assessment by Codex 2026-08-28, checked\n     4\tagainst the tree the same day. **The prototype is viable for Play and does not need a rewrite. The biggest\n     5\trisk is Play policy around Accessibility, not the dictation technology.**\n     6\t\n     7\t## FACT: what-already-satisfies-play\n     8\t`targetSdk` 36 meets Google's 2026-08-31 target-API requirement. Offline recording, transcription and\n     9\tlocal polish work on the phone. Model downloads are pinned, checksum-verified and app-private. Unit tests\n    10\tpass and `:app:assembleDebug` succeeds. The debug APK was reported at roughly 129 MB and passing 16 KB\n    11\tpage-size alignment checks (Codex, 2026-08-28, not re-measured here).\n    12\t\n    13\t## FACT: the-six-blockers\n    14\t**These are Play SUBMISSION blockers, not the whole path from stage 1.** Stage 2 acceptance in\n    15\t`../../CLAUDE.md` — fresh-phone setup and real model delivery among it — must also pass before any public\n    16\trelease. Nothing is submission-ready until all six close.\n    17\t\n    18\t1. Back-to-back insertion (#132): CLOSED 2026-09-16 on the phone receipt (4 of 4 takes, build 134) plus daily use.\n    19\t2. Accessibility disclosure and consent: BUILT (`ui/AccessibilityGuideActivity`, Agree / Not now, the\n    20\t   only route to `ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS`, verified 2026-09-15). Still owed: the review video and\n    21\t   the Console declaration form.\n    22\t3. Finish the privacy policy and the Data Safety answers. WRITTEN 2026-09-17: the Android paragraph and\n    23\t   the exact page edits (`docs/play-listing/privacy-policy-android-addendum.md`, branch\n    24\t   `feat/launch-paperwork`); the form answers (`docs/play-data-safety-answers.md`). Still owed: publish\n    25\t   the paragraph on enviouswispr.com (macOS repo website), paste the form.\n    26\t4. Model DISTRIBUTION: DONE 2026-09-17. Founder approved Cloudflare R2; the five files live on\n    27\t   `models.enviouslabs.co` (the bucket macOS and Windows already use) with Hugging Face as the fallback URL\n    28\t   (#169, `docs/play-listing/model-hosting-proposal.md`, merged 2026-09-18 after the founder's phone pass\n    29\t   on build 138). The licences are recorded and bound to their sources by\n    30\t   `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` and `ThirdPartyNoticesTest` (#15) — including Qualcomm GenieX, BSD-3-Clause\n    31\t   plus the Qualcomm Terms of Use. Owner [model-delivery.md](model-delivery.md).\n    32\t5. Signed release AAB: BUILT (`.github/workflows/play-internal.yml` signs and publishes every\n    33\t   `internal-testing` push, verified 2026-09-15). 16 KB check DONE 2026-09-17 on version 136: all 48\n    34\t   AArch64 libraries aligned; 11 Hexagon DSP files at 4 KB are never loaded by Android\n    35\t   (`docs/play-listing/16kb-page-size-check-2026-09-17.md`). Still owed: the release-flavour polish path\n    36\t   tested as itself (read the polish engine log line on the founder's next internal-build take).\n    37\t6. Complete real cross-app and multi-device testing.\n    38\t\n    39\t## FACT: the-listing-package\n    40\tWritten 2026-09-17 on `feat/launch-paperwork`, `docs/play-listing/`: `store-listing.md` (every Console\n    41\ttext field, measured lengths, a claims table naming the code owner of each sentence),\n    42\t`console-declarations.md` (foreground services, accessibility, content rating, device catalogue for\n    43\tOption A), `launch-checklist-2026-09-17.md` (the morning's order of operations), `graphics/` (512 px\n    44\ticon composed from the adaptive layers, 1024 x 500 feature graphic). Screenshots and the accessibility\n    45\treview video are captured on the emulator (see the checklist).\n    46\t\n    47\t## RULE: the-accessibility-declaration-is-the-highest-review-risk\n    48\tEnviousWispr is a general dictation app. It must **not** claim to be a disability-focused accessibility\n    49\ttool and must **not** declare `isAccessibilityTool=\"true\"`. Copying another app's declaration is what gets\n    50\ta listing rejected or pulled.\n    51\t\n    52\tGoogle requires a separate, prominent explanation immediately BEFORE sending someone to Accessibility\n    53\tsettings, followed by an affirmative choice such as Agree or Not now, plus a short review video showing the\n    54\texplanation, acceptance, refusal, and the feature working. What the explanation must say, and the rule that\n    55\tits claims must stay true in code: [`../rules/content-brand.md`](../rules/content-brand.md)\n    56\tRULE: the-accessibility-disclosure-is-policy-not-copy.\n    57\t\n    58\t**Blocker 2 has an exact scope, measured 2026-08-29: THREE unguarded hand-offs, all wired to the one\n    59\t`Settings.ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS` intent in `ui/SettingsActivity`, with no disclosure and no\n    60\tAgree/Not now.** They are the Settings \"Auto-paste access\" row, the onboarding\n    61\taccessibility step, and the Home \"Auto-paste is not connected\" card added for issue #16. The disclosure\n    62\tscreen has to sit in front of all three at once; fixing one route leaves the gap open. Nothing else in the\n    63\tapp routes there, and `shortcuts/DictationNotificationController.settingsIntent` deliberately opens the app\n    64\tinstead, so it must not be converted into a fourth.\n    65\t\n    66\t**The policy is silent on password and payment fields** — verified against the policy page 2026-08-28. It\n    67\trequires disclosure and forbids autonomous operation, and says nothing about field types. Do not add a\n    68\tfield-type refusal to satisfy a rule Google does not have.\n    69\t\n    70\t## FACT: the-account-exists-and-is-verified\n    71\tRegistered 2026-09-02 as an **Organization** account under `hello@enviouslabs.co`: publisher Envious Labs\n    72\tLLC, public developer name **Envious Labs**, developer account ID **6555143545093269704**, 25 dollar fee\n    73\tpaid. Organization and authorized-representative verification both cleared the same day. Organization tier\n    74\talso avoids the newer personal-account rule requiring 12 testers enrolled for 14 consecutive days before\n    75\tproduction access.\n    76\t\n    77\tAccount facts, the document that cleared each verification step, and the `support@enviouslabs.co` contact\n    78\tgroup live in `~/Developer/EnviousLabs/EnviousLabsLLC/04-operations/google-play.md`. Do not restate them\n    79\there.\n    80\t\n    81\t## FACT: what-a-release-build-needs-that-does-not-exist-yet\n    82\tThe project has a debug build only. A release needs a protected signing key with Play App Signing enrolled;\n    83\ta signed `.aab`, since new Play apps must use bundles; release versioning beyond `versionCode = 1`; the\n    84\tdebug and benchmark components excluded; a 16 KB compatibility check on the bundle and on a real device;\n    85\tand icon, screenshots, feature graphic, description and support email.\n    86\t\n    87\t**A release build takes a genuinely different polish path** — `polish/S1ModelSelector` reads\n    88\t`FLAG_DEBUGGABLE` — so it must be tested as itself, not inferred from the debug build.\n    89\t\n    90\t## RULE: the-listing-and-the-data-safety-form-must-match-the-code\n    91\t`privacy/PrivacyDisclosure.kt` is the source of truth for what leaves the phone. Three claims must be right\n    92\ttogether:\n    93\t\n    94\t- **Audio never leaves the phone.** True on every path today.\n    95\t- **Selected TEXT goes to a cloud provider when the user chooses one and supplies their own key.** Also\n    96\t  true, and it must appear. A listing stating only the audio claim answers the Data Safety form wrongly.\n    97\t- **NPU polish is not shipped.** Keep it out of the listing until the shipped path selects it.\n    98\t\n    99\tEvery app must provide a privacy policy and complete the Data Safety form, even one that collects nothing,\n   100\toutside an internal-only release.\n   101\t\n   102\t## RULE: device-support-is-a-decision-not-a-default\n   103\t**Decided 2026-09-16 (founder): Option A of `docs/device-support-decision.md`: 6 GB RAM minimum, Android\n   104\tGo excluded, arm64 only, no chipset restriction.** The shipped path is CPU-only, so nothing needs a\n   105\tSnapdragon. The decision changes the listing, the Console device-catalog exclusions and the test matrix;\n   106\tapply it in all three, never in one.\n   107\t\n   108\t## FACT: the-recommended-release-order\n   109\t**Decided 2026-09-16 (founder): no closed beta; go from internal testing straight to a public release.**\n   110\tOrder that remains: fix what the Play pre-launch report finds on the internal build, submit the\n   111\tAccessibility and foreground-service declarations with videos, then a limited-percentage launch, expanding\n   112\tonly once crash, insertion and download behaviour look healthy.\n   113\t\n   114\tInternal testing preparation began at the founder's request on 2026-09-12; current Console state and\n   115\tcontrol instructions live in [play-console-operations.md](play-console-operations.md). Internal testing\n   116\tcan begin before full listing setup, and exclusively internal releases do not require the Data Safety\n   117\tform. This does not establish public-release readiness or waive applicable privacy requirements.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

plan,\n    53\t                ProviderCancellation(),\n    54\t                overallTimeoutMs.coerceIn(1, KEY_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    55\t                connectTimeoutMs.coerceIn(1, Transport.Failed -> ProviderKeyCheck.Unverified(\n    60\t                when (transport.kind) {\n    61\t                    ProviderFailureKind.NETWORK -> PolishFailure.UNREACHABLE\n    62\t ProviderFailureKind.INVALID_CONFIGURATION,\n    65\t                    ProviderFailureKind.CANCELLED,\n    66\t                    ProviderFailureKind.HTTP_ERROR,\n    67\t                    ProviderFailureKind.MALFORMED_RESPONSE,\n    68\t                    ProviderFailureKind.RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE,\n    69\t                    ProviderFailureKind.REDIRECT_REJECTED,\n    70\t $provider status=${keyCheckStatus(verdict)} verdict=${verdict::class.simpleName}\")\n    78\t        return verdict\n    79\t    }\n    80\t\n    81\t    /**\n    82\t     * The live model list (#84), * for non-probe provider work in this process, all under one whole-operation deadline. Nothing here carries user content: the list has no body and the probe says \"Hi\".\n    87\t     */\n    88\t    override fun discoverModels(provider: Provider, apiKey: String): ProviderDiscovery {\n ProviderDiscovery.Refused(ProviderKeyCheck.Unverified(PolishFailure.BAD_REQUEST))\n    94\t        }\n    95\t        val deadline = System.nanoTime() + page by page for the one provider that pages.\n    99\t        val rows = mutableListOf<ListedModel>()\n   100\t        val seenCursors = HashSet<String>()\n   101\t        var pages = 0\n   102\t {"type":"item.started","item":{"id":"item_40","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc \"nl -ba docs/feature-requests/issue-194-2026-09-22-content-free-diagnostics.md | sed -n '1,126p'\"","aggregated_output":"","exit_code":null,"status":"in_progress"}}
{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_40","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc \"nl -ba docs/feature-requests/issue-194-2026-09-22-content-free-diagnostics.md | sed -n '1,126p'\"","aggregated_output":"     1\t# #194 — Local diagnostics are structurally content-free\n     2\t\n     3\t## Preface — Lane + Hardware UAT declaration\n     4\t\n     5\t**Lane:** Code\n     6\tmixed_pr: true — `Code` (`app/**`: `tests`, `codex-review`, `hardware-uat`) and `Docs/dev-tooling` (this plan, the audits, `.claude/**`: `cited-symbols` conditionally).\n     7\t\n     8\t**PAR rows closed:** none. PAR-089 (a diagnostics screen with no secrets or dictated content) is adjacent, not closed: no screen changes here.\n     9\t\n    10\t**Hardware UAT:** Y. The change touches the log calls on the heart path (capture, ASR, polish, insertion), so one ordinary take runs on the emulator after the change and its logcat is swept for the markers (a transcript fragment, an exception message) as evidence that the words never reach the log; the founder's phone is excluded by his instruction (2026-09-21 morning). What success looks like for a person: nothing they can see changes; a support engineer reading their logcat sees \"Polish failed (SocketTimeoutException at ProviderPolishClient.call:212)\", never the prompt or the words.\n    11\t\n    12\t## Preface — User Rubric\n    13\t\n    14\tUser Rubric: N/A — internal-only: no user-visible surface changes; the change is what the app writes into its own logs.\n    15\t\n    16\t## 0. TL;DR\n    17\t`DebugLogger` renders a `Throwable` as its class name and one frame location, never its message or trace; the dormant shared-storage file sink is deleted; every `${error.message}` in a diagnostic line becomes the exception's class name; the 43 raw `android.util.Log` calls in the paste package go through `DebugLogger`; the speech process's failure detail is the exception class, not its message; one JVM shape row over the source tree refuses the reintroduction of any of these, and marker rows prove the rendering.\n    18\t\n    19\t## 1. Problem\n    20\t`DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)` passes the throwable to `Log.e`, which prints `Throwable.toString()` (class plus MESSAGE) and the trace of every cause; a vendor or parser exception's message can carry the prompt, a URL with a key, or the dictated text (`code-gotchas.md` RULE: geniex-logs-the-prompt-so-silence-it-before-inference is the precedent). 29 diagnostic lines interpolate `${error.message}` directly. The off-by-default file sink writes to `/sdcard/EnviousWispr/debug.log` behind `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, which the manifest does not declare (`enableFileLogging` refuses at runtime); it has no caller and would write the same text to shared storage. 43 sites in `paste/` call `android.util.Log` directly, three of them with a throwable argument.\n    21\t\n    22\t## 2. Goals & non-goals\n    23\t### 2.1 Goals\n    24\t- Structurally: the ONLY renderer of a `Throwable` into a log line is `DebugLogger`, and it renders class + one frame, never message, `toString` or trace.\n    25\t- Every diagnostic line in `app/src/main` is free of `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `Throwable.toString` and string-templated throwables.\n    26\t- No shared-storage sink; no raw `android.util.Log` outside `DebugLogger`.\n    27\t- A static JVM row over the source tree keeps all three true; marker rows prove the renderer.\n    28\t### 2.2 Non-goals\n    29\t- Telemetry (`PayloadSanitizer`, Sentry, PostHog): unchanged, its own allowlist.\n    30\t- User-facing error text (`AppViewModel.historyError`, `DictionaryScreen`, the model-delivery notification): those read `failure.message` into a SCREEN, which is a product surface, not a diagnostic; out of scope, named in §14.\n    31\t- A closed enum of every diagnostic event name (the issue's \"closed event name\"): 180 sites of literal templates already read as shape; wrapping each in an enum member is the audit's -60 LOC budget times ten and pins nothing a shape row does not. The closed set this change enforces is the RENDERER's inputs (class name, frame, numbers, the literal template), not a catalogue of templates.\n    32\t- The vendor silencer (`S1NativeLog`, `geniex_log_silencer.cpp`): unchanged.\n    33\t\n    34\t## 2.5 Grounding brief\n    35\t\n    36\t### 1. Trace producer → owner → consumer, end to end\n    37\tProducers: 21 `DebugLogger.error` sites (`AsrService` 5, `AudioCaptureService` 7, `PolishService` 3, `SessionLog` 1, `SilenceVadService` 2, `SileroVadSession` 3), 111 `DebugLogger.warn`/`log`, 43 raw `Log.*` in `PasteAccessibilityService` (36), `RecordingAccessibilityOverlay` (5), `EditorInputSession` (2), plus the session owner's `SessionLog` seam (`DebugSessionLog` → `DebugLogger`) and `ProviderPolishClient`'s `logInfo`/`logWarn` lambdas. Owner of the rendering: `DebugLogger` (`android.util.Log` plus the file sink). Consumers: logcat (and the wispr-eyes harness, which greps template text: `Recording started`, `Take ended`, `Stopped by`, `insertion api=`), `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (a multiset of the audio service's templates), the coordinator rig's `FakeLog.awaitLine` (three literal lines). The message interpolations, measured on `69b4a6a`: 24 `DebugLogger`/`Log` lines and 5 `log.warn` lines (`grep -rn '\\.message' app/src/main/java | grep 'DebugLogger\\|Log\\.[iwe]\\|log\\.\\(warn\\|error\\|log\\)'`). One more producer of exception text into a diagnostic: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.message` as the AIDL failure `detail`, which the owner logs at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` (\"ASR failed: ... $detail\"); the legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` also carries it (a user-facing sentence on the OLD callback only; the versioned callback carries a code).\n    38\t### 2. Find the existing authority before proposing one\n    39\t`DebugLogger` is the one diagnostic owner; `SessionLog` is the owner's seam over it (#186). Both stay; the change narrows what `DebugLogger` renders. `scripts/check-visibility.py` is the precedent for a source-tree check; `SessionOwnerShapeTest.onlyTheOwnerPinsTheTarget` (an inventory of every call in production source) is the precedent for a JVM row that sweeps the tree.\n    40\t### 3. Read prior attempts and live direction\n    41\tThe catalog's `debug-local-log` row: Android's sink is off by default with no production caller and an undeclared storage requirement; macOS compiles every sink out of release; Windows ships a local log by design. No prior attempt to remove the Android sink; the audit (REF-10) asks for it.\n    42\t### 4. Name the lifecycle, trust and process boundaries a naive design would miss\n    43\tFour processes log; `DebugLogger` is an `object` per process, so the renderer is the same code everywhere. The AIDL `detail` string crosses `:asr` → main: making the PRODUCER send a class name makes every consumer content-free at once; the AIDL signature is unchanged (append-only rule untouched). The raw `Log` calls in `paste/` run in the default process's accessibility service; `DebugLogger` has no `Context` dependency, so the migration is a rename.\n    44\t### 5. Prove the high-risk premises\n    45\t- `Log.e(tag, msg, tr)` prints `tr`'s message: Android's `Log.e(String, String, Throwable)` appends `getStackTraceString(tr)`, which begins with `tr.toString()` = class + message, then every cause's. Verified by reading the platform contract; the marker row proves OUR renderer never calls it with a throwable.\n    46\t- No caller enables the file sink: `grep -rn enableFileLogging app/src` → one hit, the definition. The manifest declares no `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (grep, zero hits), so the sink could never have opened on a device.\n    47\t- The harness greps template text, not exception text: `scripts/uat/wispr_eyes.py` `last_take` reads `Stopped by ([^.]+)\\.`, `Recording started`, `insertion api=`; none of the migrated lines change a template the harness or a shape row reads (the templates stay; only `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` and the raw-Log → `DebugLogger` prefix change).\n    48\t\n    49\t## 3. Design\n    50\tD1. **`DebugLogger`** (`debug/DebugLogger.kt`): delete the file sink (`LOG_PATH`, `enableFileLogging`, `close`, `logWriter`, `writeToFile`, `truncateLog`, the `Environment`/`File`/`FileWriter` imports). `error(tag, message, throwable)` becomes `Log.e(tag, render(message, throwable))` with `render(message: String, throwable: Throwable?): String` (proposed) = `message` alone, or `\"$message ($ClassSimpleName at $frame)\"` where `frame` is the throwable's top frame as `ClassSimpleName.method:line` (or `\"no frame\"`), and the CAUSE chain contributes only class names (`caused by X, Y`), never a message. Add `debug(tag, message)` (proposed) → `Log.d` for the paste package's `Log.d` sites. `render` is `internal` so the marker rows call it on the JVM (the `Log` calls themselves are stubbed there).\n    51\tD2. **Every `${x.message}` in a diagnostic line → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}`** (29 sites, enumerated in §10). Where the same information is already carried by the throwable argument (`DebugLogger.error(TAG, \"…\", e)`) nothing changes.\n    52\tD3. **The paste package** (43 sites): `Log.i/w/d(TAG, …)` → `DebugLogger.log/warn/debug(TAG, …)`; `Log.w(TAG, \"…\", error)` (5 sites) → `DebugLogger.error(TAG, \"…\", error)`; the `import android.util.Log` lines go.\n    53\tD4. **The speech failure detail**: `AsrService.kt:133,181` send `e.javaClass.simpleName` as `detail`; the owner's line at `DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:1048` stays (it now prints a class name). The legacy callback's sentence for `AUDIO_UNREADABLE`/`DECODE_FAILED` (`detail.ifBlank { … }`) therefore reads the class name; that callback is the pre-#176 client path with no production caller (the versioned callback carries the code); stated, not redesigned.\n    54\tD5. **The static row** `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) (proposed, `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/`): reads every `app/src/main/java/**/*.kt`; (a) `import android.util.Log` appears only in `debug/DebugLogger.kt`; (b) no call to `DebugLogger.{log,warn,error,debug}`, `SessionLog`'s `log.{log,warn,error}`, or `logInfo`/`logWarn` carries `.message`, `.localizedMessage`, `stackTraceToString`, `.toString()` of a throwable or `$e`/`$error`/`$it`-templated throwable in its argument text (the call's text is the balanced-paren span from the call token, so a multi-line call is one span); (c) `DebugLogger.kt` contains no `Log.e(` / `Log.w(` call with a third argument, no `stackTraceToString`, no `Environment`, no `FileWriter`. A two-way control: the row is run once against a fixture string containing each forbidden shape and must find every one.\n    55\tD6. **Marker rows** `DebugLoggerRenderTest` (proposed): `render(\"Polish failed\", RuntimeException(\"TRANSCRIPT_MARKER https://k=SECRET\"))` contains `RuntimeException`, contains a frame token, and contains neither marker; a cause chain `IllegalStateException(\"MARKER2\")` under it contributes the class name only; `render(\"x\", null)` is `\"x\"`.\n    56\t\n    57\t## 3b. Ownership justification\n    58\tNo new owner. `DebugLogger` stays the one renderer; its surface narrows (the sink gone, `render` added, `debug` added).\n    59\t\n    60\t## 4. Contract deltas\n    61\t- `DebugLogger.enableFileLogging()` / `close()`: deleted (no caller).\n    62\t- `DebugLogger.error(tag, message, throwable)`: same signature; the rendering changes (class + frame, never message or trace).\n    63\t- `DebugLogger.debug(tag, message)`: new.\n    64\t- `IAsrCallback.onFailure(reason, detail)`: unchanged signature; `detail` now carries the exception's class name where it carried its message.\n    65\t- Log TEMPLATES: unchanged text where a template exists; the interpolated VALUE changes from a message to a class name in 29 lines.\n    66\t\n    67\t## 5. End-to-end state and lifecycle audit\n    68\tNo state. The file sink's `logWriter` lifecycle is deleted with it.\n    69\t\n    70\t## 6. Downstream consumer matrix\n    71\t- Logcat readers (a human, the harness): the same templates; a class name where a message was.\n    72\t- `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove`: its multiset lists templates with `${}` placeholders; the migrated lines keep their templates, so the row stays green (verified at build; a changed template is added there on purpose).\n    73\t- `FakeLog.awaitLine`: three literal lines, none carrying a message; unchanged.\n    74\t- The legacy `IAsrCallback` sentence: a class name instead of a message on two reasons (D4).\n    75\t- Telemetry: untouched.\n    76\t\n    77\t## 7. Failure-mode × caller table\n    78\t| Failure mode | Origin | Caller | What the user sees | Persisted | Retry |\n    79\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n    80\t| A throwable with a secret in its message reaches `DebugLogger.error` | a vendor SDK, a parser, OkHttp | any process | nothing; logcat shows the class and a frame | nothing | n/a |\n    81\t| A throwable with no stack frames (a synthetic one) | a test, a stripped build | `render` | \"(X at no frame)\" | nothing | n/a |\n    82\t| A new site interpolates `.message` | a future change | the static row | the unit suite is red with the file and line | n/a | fix the site |\n    83\t\n    84\t## 8. Caller-visible signals audit\n    85\tThe only signal that changes is the text of diagnostic lines: a class name replaces a message. Every user-facing sentence stays (`TakeNotices`, `InsertionOutcomeMessages`, the screens).\n    86\t\n    87\t## 9. Fallback source-of-truth audit\n    88\tNone: no fallback path reads a diagnostic.\n    89\t\n    90\t## 10. File-by-file changes\n    91\t- `app/src/main/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DebugLogger.kt`: D1.\n    92\t- `${x.message}` → `${x.javaClass.simpleName}` (29): `ui/PipelineBindings.kt:124`, `ui/EngineWarmUp.kt:51`, `ui/DictationSessionCoordinator.kt:277,1214,1317,1685`, `audio/DetectorFeed.kt:250,303,344`, `audio/TakeRoute.kt:113,204,245,329`, `audio/AudioCaptureService.kt:746,822,824,827,830`, `audio/WarmHoldOwner.kt:105,107,145`, `audio/PicturePublisher.kt:82,129`, `vad/SileroVadSession.kt:60`, `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt:667,688,1304,1411,1488` (these five also move to `DebugLogger`).\n    93\t- `paste/PasteAccessibilityService.kt`, `paste/RecordingAccessibilityOverlay.kt`, `paste/EditorInputSession.kt`: D3.\n    94\t- `asr/AsrService.kt:133,181`: D4.\n    95\t- `app/src/test/java/com/envi/wispr/debug/DiagnosticsShapeTest.kt` (proposed, new), `DebugLoggerRenderTest.kt` (new).\n    96\t- `docs/audits/2026-09-22-194-*.md`; the catalog's `debug-local-log` Android row (the sink is gone).\n    97\t\n    98\t## 11. Testing\n    99\t1. Classes: `DebugLoggerRenderTest` is Product Outcome for the PRIVACY promise (`testing-philosophy.md` RULE: a-public-product-promise-needs-a-binding-test: the Data Safety form says no dictated content leaves the device, and a logcat line is readable by any app holding the platform's read-logs permission on a debug device and by anyone with the phone on a cable); `DiagnosticsShapeTest` (proposed) is a Drift Guard with a two-way control.\n   100\t2. Reverts: §11.2.\n   101\t3. Not tested: the vendor SDKs' own logging (owned by the silencer).\n   102\t### 11.1 Hardware UAT spec\n   103\tEmulator, wispr-eyes, debug build: one ordinary spoken take into Gmail (`dictate_emulator`) after the change; then `logcat -d` is swept for the dictated sentence's words and for `Exception:` followed by a colon-and-message shape from our tags (`DictationSession`, `AudioCapture`, `AsrService`, `PolishService`, `PasteService`): zero hits from our tags is the pass; the harness's own `insertion api=` and `Recording started` lines must still be found (the templates survived). The founder's phone: NOT RUN.\n   104\t### 11.2 Other obligations\n   105\t| Test | Class | Proves | Revert that turns it red |\n   106\t|---|---|---|---|\n   107\t| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.aThrowablesMessageNeverReachesTheLine` (proposed) | Product Outcome | the marker in the message and in a cause's message is absent; the class names and a frame are present | render `throwable.toString()` |\n   108\t| `DebugLoggerRenderTest.noThrowableRendersTheMessageAlone` (proposed) | Drift Guard | `render(\"x\", null) == \"x\"` | append a suffix |\n   109\t| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.onlyTheDiagnosticOwnerImportsAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | one file imports `android.util.Log` | add an import elsewhere |\n   110\t| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.noDiagnosticLineCarriesExceptionText` (proposed) | Drift Guard | zero forbidden shapes in any diagnostic call span; the fixture control finds all of them | reintroduce one `${e.message}` |\n   111\t| `DiagnosticsShapeTest.theOwnerNeverHandsAThrowableToAndroidLog` (proposed) | Drift Guard | no three-argument `Log.e`/`Log.w`, no `stackTraceToString`, no file sink symbols in `DebugLogger.kt` | restore `Log.e(tag, message, throwable)` |\n   112\t| `AudioServiceShapeTest.everyLogTemplateAndThreadNameSurvivesTheMove` (exists) | Drift Guard | the audio templates unchanged | change a template |\n   113\t\n   114\t## 12. Blast radius & rollback\n   115\tEvery diagnostic line in four processes; no user-visible surface; no data. Rollback is the PR revert.\n   116\t\n   117\t## 13. Ship criteria specific to THIS change\n   118\t- Every row in §11.2 green; receipts red; the static row's two-way control found every fixture shape.\n   119\t- The emulator take's logcat sweep: zero content or exception-message hits from our tags; the templates the harness reads still present.\n   120\t- Codex all-clear with a confirming rerun.\n   121\t\n   122\t## 14. Open questions\n   123\t- The user-facing `failure.message` readers (`AppViewModel`, `DictionaryScreen`, `ModelDeliveryWorker`'s notification) show an exception's text ON SCREEN; that is a product-copy question (which sentence should a user see when history or the word list cannot be read), not a diagnostics one, and is not changed here; a follow-up issue is filed if Codex or the founder want it.\n   124\t\n   125\t## 15. Related\n   126\tREF-10 in `docs/audits/2026-09-20-senior-audit.md`; #176 (the typed ASR failure); `kotlin-patterns.md` RULE: no-content-in-diagnostics.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
